Yeah, um… I came in because things have been kind of falling apart since I moved into this busted-up room I’m staying in. Like, it’s not just annoying stuff, it’s gotten to where I’m snapping at people over little things, and I’m not usually like that.

I’ll be up half the night trying to deal with the place — leaks, heat not working right, whatever breaks next — and then I’m dragging myself into work late. I do construction, so being tired and off like that, it matters. I’ve been messing up stuff I normally handle fine, and then I get more pissed at myself, and it just keeps piling up. Yeah… on edge is probably a better way to put it than sad. Since I moved into that room, I feel like I’m always kind of braced for the next thing to go wrong. Like I’ll get home from work and instead of relaxing, I’m checking if something’s leaking again, or if the heat’s acting up, or if I’m gonna have to spend half the night trying to patch something just so I can sleep.

I do construction, so if I’m dragging or my head’s somewhere else, it shows. I’ve been messing up things I usually wouldn’t mess up, and then I get even more wound up about that. So yeah, I guess I’ve been pretty anxious or keyed up — mostly because it feels like I can’t ever just settle down.

I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.

It’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.

I guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.

So yeah, I guess I’ve been pretty anxious or keyed up — mostly because it feels like I can’t ever just settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I haven’t really been paying attention to “enjoying things” the way I normally would. Like, I know that sounds weird, but lately it’s been more like I get through work, deal with whatever’s in front of me, and then the day’s kind of gone.\n\nIf you mean like hobbies or hanging out or stuff I usually do to relax… I guess I haven’t thought about it enough to say it’s gone or anything. It’s more that everything’s been so wrapped up in getting through the day that I’m not really checking in with myself like, “Am I having fun?” You know? So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I mean, I haven’t really been paying attention to “enjoying things” the way I normally would. Like, I know that sounds weird, but lately it’s been more like I get through work, deal with whatever’s in front of me, and then the day’s kind of gone.\n\nIf you mean like hobbies or hanging out or stuff I usually do to relax… I guess I haven’t thought about it enough to say it’s gone or anything. It’s more that everything’s been so wrapped up in getting through the day that I’m not really checking in with myself like, “Am I having fun?” You know? So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself or anything like that, so I couldn’t tell you a number. My clothes don’t feel really different, like I haven’t had to tighten my belt a bunch or noticed my pants getting loose or too tight.\n\nI guess with all the stuff going on, I’m not paying super close attention to that kind of thing. I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.

Like at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.

And the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. Then I mess up things I usually handle fine, and that makes me even more irritated, because I know I can do better than that.

So yeah, I’d say I’m getting angry easier. Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.

I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… not really in a clear way, I guess. I haven’t been thinking like, “I’m not going there because of how I feel,” you know? It’s not like I’m scared of certain places or people or anything like that.

I mean, I probably keep more to myself lately just because I’m tired of dealing with everything and I don’t always feel like being around people, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding specific places or activities on purpose. If I need to go somewhere, I go. If I need to deal with somebody, I deal with them. It’s more like I’m just not as up for extra stuff.

Like if someone wanted to hang out, I might not be in the mood, but I don’t know if that counts as avoiding. It’s not really because of the place or the person. It’s more just… I don’t have the energy for it sometimes. But no, I haven’t noticed myself staying away from certain places or people in any big way.

Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get really worked up or stressed, especially when I’m dealing with the room and it’s just one thing after another, but I don’t know that I’d call it a panic attack.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean full-on out of nowhere, heart racing, can’t calm down kind of thing, then no, I haven’t really had that. It’s more just being tense and frustrated, and then I try to get through whatever’s happening. I haven’t had some big episode where I thought I needed to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a clear way, I guess. I haven’t been thinking like, “I’m not going there because of how I feel,” you know? It’s not like I’m scared of certain places or people or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I probably keep more to myself lately just because I’m tired of dealing with everything and I don’t always feel like being around people, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding specific places or activities on purpose. If I need to go somewhere, I go. If I need to deal with somebody, I deal with them. It’s more like I’m just not as up for extra stuff.\n\nLike if someone wanted to hang out, I might not be in the mood, but I don’t know if that counts as avoiding. It’s not really because of the place or the person. It’s more just… I don’t have the energy for it sometimes. But no, I haven’t noticed myself staying away from certain places or people in any big way.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been feeling guilty or worthless about things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… guilty, maybe a little here and there, but not really “worthless,” if that makes sense. Like, I’ll feel bad when I’m not handling things the way I usually would, or if I snap at somebody and then later I’m thinking, “Why did I say it like that?” But I don’t think I’m walking around feeling like I’m a bad person or like I’m worth nothing.

It’s more like I get frustrated with myself. I’ll think, “Come on, you should be dealing with this better,” especially over stuff that maybe normally wouldn’t get to me as much. But I can usually tell it’s because I’m stressed and worn down, not because I actually believe I’m useless or anything.

So… yeah, some guilt about how I’m acting sometimes, but not some deep feeling of being worthless.

I do construction, so being tired and off like that, it matters. I’ve been messing up stuff I normally handle fine, and then I get more pissed at myself, and it just keeps piling up. I guess I finally figured I should talk to somebody before I make things worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Like, I’m not walking around crying or thinking, “I’m depressed,” you know? It’s more like I’m worn down and irritated a lot, and then I get stuck in my head about the place and how I ended up there.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of low, I guess, especially when I come back and something else is wrong or I realize I’m going to have to deal with the same mess again. But “sad most of the time”… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means like sad all day every day. It’s more like I’m just tired of everything being a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge is probably a better way to put it than sad. Since I moved into that room, I feel like I’m always kind of braced for the next thing to go wrong. Like I’ll get home from work and instead of relaxing, I’m checking if something’s leaking again, or if the heat’s acting up, or if I’m gonna have to spend half the night trying to patch something just so I can sleep.\n\nAnd then when I don’t sleep right, I go into work already irritated and tired, and I’m late sometimes because I was dealing with the room. I do construction, so if I’m dragging or my head’s somewhere else, it shows. I’ve been messing up things I usually wouldn’t mess up, and then I get even more wound up about that. So yeah, I guess I’ve been pretty anxious or keyed up — mostly because it feels like I can’t ever just settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I haven’t really been paying attention to “enjoying things” the way I normally would. Like, I know that sounds weird, but lately it’s been more like I get through work, deal with whatever’s in front of me, and then the day’s kind of gone.\n\nIf you mean like hobbies or hanging out or stuff I usually do to relax… I guess I haven’t thought about it enough to say it’s gone or anything. It’s more that everything’s been so wrapped up in getting through the day that I’m not really checking in with myself like, “Am I having fun?” You know? So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. But no, I haven’t noticed any big change in my weight since moving in there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting angry or irritated more easily than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.\n\nLike at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.\n\nAnd the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… hopeless is a strong word. I wouldn’t say I feel like there’s no future or nothing can ever get better. It’s more like I get worn down by it and I start thinking, “How long is this going to keep being like this?” or “Why does every little thing have to be such a hassle?” But that’s different than feeling like my life is over or there’s no point.

I guess in the moment, when I’m dealing with the room situation and I’m already tired, it can feel like I can’t see the end of it. Like I know logically it probably won’t be forever, but when you’re in it, it just feels heavy and annoying and like I don’t have much control over it. So I get discouraged, yeah.

But hopeless about the future overall? No, I don’t think so. I still figure things can change or I can get through it somehow. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.

My clothes don’t feel really different, like I haven’t had to tighten my belt a bunch or noticed my pants getting loose or too tight.\n\nI guess with all the stuff going on, I’m not paying super close attention to that kind of thing. I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. But no, I haven’t noticed any big change in my weight since moving in there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting angry or irritated more easily than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.\n\nLike at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.\n\nAnd the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. Then I mess up things I usually handle fine, and that makes me even more irritated, because I know I can do better than that.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say I’m getting angry easier. Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get really worked up or stressed, especially when I’m dealing with the room and it’s just one thing after another, but I don’t know that I’d call it a panic attack.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I wouldn’t say I wake up every single day feeling depressed or anything that clear-cut. Some days I’m just kind of flat, or irritated, or tired of dealing with the same stuff, and then other days I’m more okay once I get moving.

No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.

I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself or anything like that, so I couldn’t tell you a number. My clothes don’t feel really different, like I haven’t had to tighten my belt a bunch or noticed my pants getting loose or too tight.\n\nI guess with all the stuff going on, I’m not paying super close attention to that kind of thing. I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. But no, I haven’t noticed any big change in my weight since moving in there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting angry or irritated more easily than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.\n\nLike at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.\n\nAnd the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. Then I mess up things I usually handle fine, and that makes me even more irritated, because I know I can do better than that.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say I’m getting angry easier. Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get really worked up or stressed, especially when I’m dealing with the room and it’s just one thing after another, but I don’t know that I’d call it a panic attack.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean full-on out of nowhere, heart racing, can’t calm down kind of thing, then no, I haven’t really had that. It’s more just being tense and frustrated, and then I try to get through whatever’s happening. I haven’t had some big episode where I thought I needed to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a clear way, I guess. I haven’t been thinking like, “I’m not going there because of how I feel,” you know? It’s not like I’m scared of certain places or people or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I probably keep more to myself lately just because I’m tired of dealing with everything and I don’t always feel like being around people, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding specific places or activities on purpose. If I need to go somewhere, I go. If I need to deal with somebody, I deal with them. It’s more like I’m just not as up for extra stuff.\n\nLike if someone wanted to hang out, I might not be in the mood, but I don’t know if that counts as avoiding. It’s not really because of the place or the person. It’s more just… I don’t have the energy for it sometimes. But no, I haven’t noticed myself staying away from certain places or people in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… guilty, maybe a little here and there, but not really “worthless,” if that makes sense. Like, I’ll feel bad when I’m not handling things the way I usually would, or if I snap at somebody and then later I’m thinking, “Why did I say it like that?” But I don’t think I’m walking around feeling like I’m a bad person or like I’m worth nothing.\n\nIt’s more like I get frustrated with myself. I’ll think, “Come on, you should be dealing with this better,” especially over stuff that maybe normally wouldn’t get to me as much. But I can usually tell it’s because I’m stressed and worn down, not because I actually believe I’m useless or anything.\n\nSo… yeah, some guilt about how I’m acting sometimes, but not some deep feeling of being worthless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation or the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… hopeless is a strong word. I wouldn’t say I feel like there’s no future or nothing can ever get better. It’s more like I get worn down by it and I start thinking, “How long is this going to keep being like this?” or “Why does every little thing have to be such a hassle?” But that’s different than feeling like my life is over or there’s no point.\n\nI guess in the moment, when I’m dealing with the room situation and I’m already tired, it can feel like I can’t see the end of it. Like I know logically it probably won’t be forever, but when you’re in it, it just feels heavy and annoying and like I don’t have much control over it. So I get discouraged, yeah.\n\nBut hopeless about the future overall? No, I don’t think so. I still figure things can change or I can get through it somehow. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I wouldn’t say I wake up every single day feeling depressed or anything that clear-cut. Some days I’m just kind of flat, or irritated, or tired of dealing with the same stuff, and then other days I’m more okay once I get moving.\n\nSo… low mood most days? Maybe not “most days” in the way you mean it. I have stretches where I feel down or just not like myself, but it isn’t like I’m constantly sad all day every day. It kind of comes and goes depending on how the day is going and what I’m dealing with. Sometimes it’s more frustration than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not as upbeat as I usually am, but I don’t know if I’d call it a low mood most days. It’s more like I’m worn down, and some days that turns into feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be okay for a while, then something gets to me and I feel low or irritated or just drained. And then later I might feel a little more normal again, depending on what’s going on. So it’s not steady like that. I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I wouldn’t say I wake up every single day feeling depressed or anything that clear-cut. Some days I’m just kind of flat, or irritated, or tired of dealing with the same stuff, and then other days I’m more okay once I get moving.\n\nSo… low mood most days? Maybe not “most days” in the way you mean it. I have stretches where I feel down or just not like myself, but it isn’t like I’m constantly sad all day every day. It kind of comes and goes depending on how the day is going and what I’m dealing with. Sometimes it’s more frustration than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not as upbeat as I usually am, but I don’t know if I’d call it a low mood most days. It’s more like I’m worn down, and some days that turns into feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be okay for a while, then something gets to me and I feel low or irritated or just drained. And then later I might feel a little more normal again, depending on what’s going on. So it’s not steady like that. I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things since you moved into the new room?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / construction_laborer / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response\nmanifestation: \"I came in because ever since I moved into that busted-up room, I’m snapping at people, staying up fixing leaks or heat, then dragging myself late to work and messing up stuff I normally handle.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. Not like I can’t focus on anything at all, but since I moved into that room, I’m definitely more scattered. It’s like my head is already full before I even get to work.

I’ll be at the job and normally I know what I’m doing, you know? I’ve done construction labor long enough that there’s stuff I can usually handle without thinking too hard. But lately I’ll mess up little things, or I’ll have to double-check what someone told me because I wasn’t really taking it in the first time. And then I get frustrated with myself because it’s not like me.

A lot of it is that I’m up late dealing with that room. Like if there’s a leak or the heat’s acting up, I end up trying to fix it or at least keep it from getting worse, and then I’m dragging the next morning. I’ve been late to work because of it too, which just makes me more stressed. Then I’m already irritated, and if someone says something to me wrong, I snap quicker than I normally would.

So yeah, focusing has been harder, but I feel like it’s tied to the whole situation with the room wearing me down. It’s not like I suddenly forgot how to do my job. I’m just tired and pissed off and distracted by all this stuff I shouldn’t have to be dealing with.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F41.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I came in because things have been kind of falling apart since I moved into this busted-up room I’m staying in. Like, it’s not just annoying stuff, it’s gotten to where I’m snapping at people over little things, and I’m not usually like that.\n\nI’ll be up half the night trying to deal with the place — leaks, heat not working right, whatever breaks next — and then I’m dragging myself into work late. I do construction, so being tired and off like that, it matters. I’ve been messing up stuff I normally handle fine, and then I get more pissed at myself, and it just keeps piling up. I guess I finally figured I should talk to somebody before I make things worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Like, I’m not walking around crying or thinking, “I’m depressed,” you know? It’s more like I’m worn down and irritated a lot, and then I get stuck in my head about the place and how I ended up there.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of low, I guess, especially when I come back and something else is wrong or I realize I’m going to have to deal with the same mess again. But “sad most of the time”… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means like sad all day every day. It’s more like I’m just tired of everything being a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge is probably a better way to put it than sad. Since I moved into that room, I feel like I’m always kind of braced for the next thing to go wrong. Like I’ll get home from work and instead of relaxing, I’m checking if something’s leaking again, or if the heat’s acting up, or if I’m gonna have to spend half the night trying to patch something just so I can sleep.\n\nAnd then when I don’t sleep right, I go into work already irritated and tired, and I’m late sometimes because I was dealing with the room. I do construction, so if I’m dragging or my head’s somewhere else, it shows. I’ve been messing up things I usually wouldn’t mess up, and then I get even more wound up about that. So yeah, I guess I’ve been pretty anxious or keyed up — mostly because it feels like I can’t ever just settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I haven’t really been paying attention to “enjoying things” the way I normally would. Like, I know that sounds weird, but lately it’s been more like I get through work, deal with whatever’s in front of me, and then the day’s kind of gone.\n\nIf you mean like hobbies or hanging out or stuff I usually do to relax… I guess I haven’t thought about it enough to say it’s gone or anything. It’s more that everything’s been so wrapped up in getting through the day that I’m not really checking in with myself like, “Am I having fun?” You know? So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself or anything like that, so I couldn’t tell you a number. My clothes don’t feel really different, like I haven’t had to tighten my belt a bunch or noticed my pants getting loose or too tight.\n\nI guess with all the stuff going on, I’m not paying super close attention to that kind of thing. I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. But no, I haven’t noticed any big change in my weight since moving in there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting angry or irritated more easily than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.\n\nLike at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.\n\nAnd the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. Then I mess up things I usually handle fine, and that makes me even more irritated, because I know I can do better than that.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say I’m getting angry easier. Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get really worked up or stressed, especially when I’m dealing with the room and it’s just one thing after another, but I don’t know that I’d call it a panic attack.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean full-on out of nowhere, heart racing, can’t calm down kind of thing, then no, I haven’t really had that. It’s more just being tense and frustrated, and then I try to get through whatever’s happening. I haven’t had some big episode where I thought I needed to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a clear way, I guess. I haven’t been thinking like, “I’m not going there because of how I feel,” you know? It’s not like I’m scared of certain places or people or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I probably keep more to myself lately just because I’m tired of dealing with everything and I don’t always feel like being around people, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding specific places or activities on purpose. If I need to go somewhere, I go. If I need to deal with somebody, I deal with them. It’s more like I’m just not as up for extra stuff.\n\nLike if someone wanted to hang out, I might not be in the mood, but I don’t know if that counts as avoiding. It’s not really because of the place or the person. It’s more just… I don’t have the energy for it sometimes. But no, I haven’t noticed myself staying away from certain places or people in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… guilty, maybe a little here and there, but not really “worthless,” if that makes sense. Like, I’ll feel bad when I’m not handling things the way I usually would, or if I snap at somebody and then later I’m thinking, “Why did I say it like that?” But I don’t think I’m walking around feeling like I’m a bad person or like I’m worth nothing.\n\nIt’s more like I get frustrated with myself. I’ll think, “Come on, you should be dealing with this better,” especially over stuff that maybe normally wouldn’t get to me as much. But I can usually tell it’s because I’m stressed and worn down, not because I actually believe I’m useless or anything.\n\nSo… yeah, some guilt about how I’m acting sometimes, but not some deep feeling of being worthless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation or the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… hopeless is a strong word. I wouldn’t say I feel like there’s no future or nothing can ever get better. It’s more like I get worn down by it and I start thinking, “How long is this going to keep being like this?” or “Why does every little thing have to be such a hassle?” But that’s different than feeling like my life is over or there’s no point.\n\nI guess in the moment, when I’m dealing with the room situation and I’m already tired, it can feel like I can’t see the end of it. Like I know logically it probably won’t be forever, but when you’re in it, it just feels heavy and annoying and like I don’t have much control over it. So I get discouraged, yeah.\n\nBut hopeless about the future overall? No, I don’t think so. I still figure things can change or I can get through it somehow. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like I know logically it probably won’t be forever, but when you’re in it, it just feels heavy and annoying and like I don’t have much control over it. So I get discouraged, yeah.\n\nBut hopeless about the future overall? No, I don’t think so. I still figure things can change or I can get through it somehow. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I wouldn’t say I wake up every single day feeling depressed or anything that clear-cut. Some days I’m just kind of flat, or irritated, or tired of dealing with the same stuff, and then other days I’m more okay once I get moving.\n\nSo… low mood most days? Maybe not “most days” in the way you mean it. I have stretches where I feel down or just not like myself, but it isn’t like I’m constantly sad all day every day. It kind of comes and goes depending on how the day is going and what I’m dealing with. Sometimes it’s more frustration than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not as upbeat as I usually am, but I don’t know if I’d call it a low mood most days. It’s more like I’m worn down, and some days that turns into feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be okay for a while, then something gets to me and I feel low or irritated or just drained. And then later I might feel a little more normal again, depending on what’s going on. So it’s not steady like that. I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I can’t focus on anything at all, but since I moved into that room, I’m definitely more scattered. It’s like my head is already full before I even get to work.\n\nI’ll be at the job and normally I know what I’m doing, you know? I’ve done construction labor long enough that there’s stuff I can usually handle without thinking too hard. And then I get frustrated with myself because it’s not like me.\n\nA lot of it is that I’m up late dealing with that room. I’ve been late to work because of it too, which just makes me more stressed. Then I’m already irritated, and if someone says something to me wrong, I snap quicker than I normally would.\n\nSo yeah, focusing has been harder, but I feel like it’s tied to the whole situation with the room wearing me down. I don’t really know how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I still like the things I like, if that makes sense. It’s not like I suddenly hate everything or nothing feels good at all. But I guess I haven’t really been doing as much, or when I do, I’m kind of half in it.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… not completely, no. I wouldn’t say I’m “no longer able” to enjoy things. That sounds kind of bigger than what I mean. Like, if something actually grabs my attention, I can still laugh or get into it for a little bit, or feel like, okay, this is nice.

But it’s just not as easy lately. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and I’m kind of just sitting there, not really into it, but I don’t know if that’s because I can’t enjoy it or because my head’s somewhere else. So I guess I’d say the pleasure is still there, just not as strong or not as steady as it used to be. It kind of comes and goes.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F41.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I came in because things have been kind of falling apart since I moved into this busted-up room I’m staying in. Like, it’s not just annoying stuff, it’s gotten to where I’m snapping at people over little things, and I’m not usually like that.\n\nI’ll be up half the night trying to deal with the place — leaks, heat not working right, whatever breaks next — and then I’m dragging myself into work late. I do construction, so being tired and off like that, it matters. I’ve been messing up stuff I normally handle fine, and then I get more pissed at myself, and it just keeps piling up. I guess I finally figured I should talk to somebody before I make things worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Like, I’m not walking around crying or thinking, “I’m depressed,” you know? It’s more like I’m worn down and irritated a lot, and then I get stuck in my head about the place and how I ended up there.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of low, I guess, especially when I come back and something else is wrong or I realize I’m going to have to deal with the same mess again. But “sad most of the time”… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means like sad all day every day. It’s more like I’m just tired of everything being a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge is probably a better way to put it than sad. Since I moved into that room, I feel like I’m always kind of braced for the next thing to go wrong. Like I’ll get home from work and instead of relaxing, I’m checking if something’s leaking again, or if the heat’s acting up, or if I’m gonna have to spend half the night trying to patch something just so I can sleep.\n\nAnd then when I don’t sleep right, I go into work already irritated and tired, and I’m late sometimes because I was dealing with the room. I do construction, so if I’m dragging or my head’s somewhere else, it shows. I’ve been messing up things I usually wouldn’t mess up, and then I get even more wound up about that. So yeah, I guess I’ve been pretty anxious or keyed up — mostly because it feels like I can’t ever just settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, I haven’t really been paying attention to “enjoying things” the way I normally would. Like, I know that sounds weird, but lately it’s been more like I get through work, deal with whatever’s in front of me, and then the day’s kind of gone.\n\nIf you mean like hobbies or hanging out or stuff I usually do to relax… I guess I haven’t thought about it enough to say it’s gone or anything. It’s more that everything’s been so wrapped up in getting through the day that I’m not really checking in with myself like, “Am I having fun?” You know? So… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say I noticed some big change exactly, but I also don’t feel like I’ve had much room to enjoy much.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say I’ve “lost interest,” exactly. Like, if you’re asking whether the stuff I used to like suddenly feels pointless or I don’t care about it anymore… I’m not sure that’s what’s happening.\n\nIt’s more like I haven’t really been in the position to get into anything. By the time I think about doing something I’d normally enjoy, I’m already kind of tired or distracted, and then I’m like, “Forget it, I’ll just sit down for a minute,” and then the night’s gone. So I haven’t really tested it, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I still think I’d enjoy those things if I could actually relax enough to do them. I just haven’t been doing much of that lately. So… no, I wouldn’t say I’ve clearly lost interest. More like it’s been pushed to the side.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so? I mean, I haven’t been weighing myself or anything like that, so I couldn’t tell you a number. My clothes don’t feel really different, like I haven’t had to tighten my belt a bunch or noticed my pants getting loose or too tight.\n\nI guess with all the stuff going on, I’m not paying super close attention to that kind of thing. I’m eating when I eat, getting through the day, you know. But no, I haven’t noticed any big change in my weight since moving in there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself getting angry or irritated more easily than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. That’s honestly part of why I came in, because I don’t feel like I’m normally like that. Since I moved into that busted-up room, I feel like I’m snapping at people over stuff I probably would’ve let go before.\n\nLike at work, somebody says something small or asks me to redo something, and I can feel myself getting hot right away, like I’m already irritated before I even think about it. And then I’ll say something sharper than I mean to. I’m a construction laborer, so people talk rough sometimes, I get that, but this is different. It’s like I don’t have the same buffer I used to have.\n\nAnd the room situation just keeps setting me off too. I’m up dealing with leaks or the heat not working right, trying to patch things or figure out what’s going on, and then I don’t get enough rest, and the next morning I’m dragging myself in late or I’m not on my game. Then I mess up things I usually handle fine, and that makes me even more irritated, because I know I can do better than that.\n\nSo yeah, I’d say I’m getting angry easier. Not like I’m looking to fight people or anything, but I’m definitely more short-tempered than I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any panic attacks since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way I hear people talk about panic attacks, like suddenly feeling like you’re dying or can’t breathe or you’re about to pass out. I mean, I’ve had moments where I get really worked up or stressed, especially when I’m dealing with the room and it’s just one thing after another, but I don’t know that I’d call it a panic attack.\n\nI guess I’m not totally sure what counts, though. Like, if you mean full-on out of nowhere, heart racing, can’t calm down kind of thing, then no, I haven’t really had that. It’s more just being tense and frustrated, and then I try to get through whatever’s happening. I haven’t had some big episode where I thought I needed to go to the hospital or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling since moving into the new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a clear way, I guess. I haven’t been thinking like, “I’m not going there because of how I feel,” you know? It’s not like I’m scared of certain places or people or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I probably keep more to myself lately just because I’m tired of dealing with everything and I don’t always feel like being around people, but I wouldn’t say I’m avoiding specific places or activities on purpose. If I need to go somewhere, I go. If I need to deal with somebody, I deal with them. It’s more like I’m just not as up for extra stuff.\n\nLike if someone wanted to hang out, I might not be in the mood, but I don’t know if that counts as avoiding. It’s not really because of the place or the person. It’s more just… I don’t have the energy for it sometimes. But no, I haven’t noticed myself staying away from certain places or people in any big way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about things that wouldn't normally bother you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… guilty, maybe a little here and there, but not really “worthless,” if that makes sense. Like, I’ll feel bad when I’m not handling things the way I usually would, or if I snap at somebody and then later I’m thinking, “Why did I say it like that?” But I don’t think I’m walking around feeling like I’m a bad person or like I’m worth nothing.\n\nIt’s more like I get frustrated with myself. I’ll think, “Come on, you should be dealing with this better,” especially over stuff that maybe normally wouldn’t get to me as much. But I can usually tell it’s because I’m stressed and worn down, not because I actually believe I’m useless or anything.\n\nSo… yeah, some guilt about how I’m acting sometimes, but not some deep feeling of being worthless.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless about your situation or the future?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… hopeless is a strong word. I wouldn’t say I feel like there’s no future or nothing can ever get better. It’s more like I get worn down by it and I start thinking, “How long is this going to keep being like this?” or “Why does every little thing have to be such a hassle?” But that’s different than feeling like my life is over or there’s no point.\n\nI guess in the moment, when I’m dealing with the room situation and I’m already tired, it can feel like I can’t see the end of it. Like I know logically it probably won’t be forever, but when you’re in it, it just feels heavy and annoying and like I don’t have much control over it. So I get discouraged, yeah.\n\nBut hopeless about the future overall? No, I don’t think so. I still figure things can change or I can get through it somehow. I just don’t always feel very positive about it day to day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like, I wouldn’t say I wake up every single day feeling depressed or anything that clear-cut. Some days I’m just kind of flat, or irritated, or tired of dealing with the same stuff, and then other days I’m more okay once I get moving.\n\nSo… low mood most days? Maybe not “most days” in the way you mean it. I have stretches where I feel down or just not like myself, but it isn’t like I’m constantly sad all day every day. It kind of comes and goes depending on how the day is going and what I’m dealing with. Sometimes it’s more frustration than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not as upbeat as I usually am, but I don’t know if I’d call it a low mood most days. It’s more like I’m worn down, and some days that turns into feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be okay for a while, then something gets to me and I feel low or irritated or just drained. And then later I might feel a little more normal again, depending on what’s going on. So it’s not steady like that. I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I can’t focus on anything at all, but since I moved into that room, I’m definitely more scattered. It’s like my head is already full before I even get to work.\n\nI’ll be at the job and normally I know what I’m doing, you know? I’ve done construction labor long enough that there’s stuff I can usually handle without thinking too hard. But lately I’ll mess up little things, or I’ll have to double-check what someone told me because I wasn’t really taking it in the first time. And then I get frustrated with myself because it’s not like me.\n\nA lot of it is that I’m up late dealing with that room. Like if there’s a leak or the heat’s acting up, I end up trying to fix it or at least keep it from getting worse, and then I’m dragging the next morning. I’ve been late to work because of it too, which just makes me more stressed. Then I’m already irritated, and if someone says something to me wrong, I snap quicker than I normally would.\n\nSo yeah, focusing has been harder, but I feel like it’s tied to the whole situation with the room wearing me down. It’s not like I suddenly forgot how to do my job. I’m just tired and pissed off and distracted by all this stuff I shouldn’t have to be dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking pleasure in things that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that one cleanly. I mean, I still like the things I like, if that makes sense. It’s not like I suddenly hate everything or nothing feels good at all. But I guess I haven’t really been doing as much, or when I do, I’m kind of half in it.\n\nLike if I’m watching something or trying to relax, sometimes I can enjoy it for a bit, but then my mind kind of drifts back to everything else and it doesn’t really feel as easy to just sit there and enjoy myself. But I don’t know if that’s the same as not taking pleasure in things. It’s more like I’m distracted or not in the mood a lot of the time.\n\nSo… I’d say I still can enjoy stuff, just maybe not as much or not as often lately. It depends on the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to derive pleasure from activities that you once found enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely, no. I wouldn’t say I’m “no longer able” to enjoy things. That sounds kind of bigger than what I mean. Like, if something actually grabs my attention, I can still laugh or get into it for a little bit, or feel like, okay, this is nice.\n\nBut it’s just not as easy lately. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and I’m kind of just sitting there, not really into it, but I don’t know if that’s because I can’t enjoy it or because my head’s somewhere else. So I guess I’d say the pleasure is still there, just not as strong or not as steady as it used to be. It kind of comes and goes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Sometimes it’s more frustration than sadness, if that makes sense.\n\nI guess I’ve noticed I’m not as upbeat as I usually am, but I don’t know if I’d call it a low mood most days. It’s more like I’m worn down, and some days that turns into feeling down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day, every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s like that. Not most of the day, every day. I mean, I definitely have times where I’m in a bad mood or I feel down, and sometimes it kind of hangs around longer than I want it to, but it’s not like I’m waking up sad and staying that way until I go to sleep every single day.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll be okay for a while, then something gets to me and I feel low or irritated or just drained. And then later I might feel a little more normal again, depending on what’s going on. So it’s not steady like that. I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. It’s like my head is already full before I even get to work.\n\nI’ll be at the job and normally I know what I’m doing, you know? I’ve done construction labor long enough that there’s stuff I can usually handle without thinking too hard. But lately I’ll mess up little things, or I’ll have to double-check what someone told me because I wasn’t really taking it in the first time. I’ve been late to work because of it too, which just makes me more stressed. Then I’m already irritated, and if someone says something to me wrong, I snap quicker than I normally would.\n\nSo yeah, focusing has been harder, but I feel like it’s tied to the whole situation with the room wearing me down. It’s not like I suddenly forgot how to do my job. I’m just tired and pissed off and distracted by all this stuff I shouldn’t have to be dealing with.\n\nDoctor: Have you been taking pleasure in things that you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that one cleanly. But I guess I haven’t really been doing as much, or when I do, I’m kind of half in it.\n\nLike if I’m watching something or trying to relax, sometimes I can enjoy it for a bit, but then my mind kind of drifts back to everything else and it doesn’t really feel as easy to just sit there and enjoy myself. I don’t know if I’d say most of every day. I mean, I’ve been down, yeah, but it’s not like I wake up and every single hour is just low the whole time. It kind of depends what’s going on, you know?

I’m not saying I’m totally fine, because I’m not really feeling like myself, but “most of the day, every day” sounds stronger than what I’d say is happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble focusing or concentrating on things since you moved into the new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like I can’t focus on anything at all, but since I moved into that room, I’m definitely more scattered. It’s like my head is already full before I even get to work.\n\nI’ll be at the job and normally I know what I’m doing, you know? I’ve done construction labor long enough that there’s stuff I can usually handle without thinking too hard. I guess I’d say my mood’s been worse than normal, but I’m not sure it’s all day, every day.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 26 / construction_laborer / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I’m not sure. I do think ahead about stuff, yeah, but I don’t know if I’d call it “often” or like worrying all the time about the future. Sometimes I’ll catch myself going over things in my head, like what if this happens or what if that doesn’t work out, but other times I’m just dealing with whatever’s right in front of me.

So I guess… yes and no? I wouldn’t say I’m totally carefree or anything, but I also don’t know if it’s more than what anybody would do when things feel uncertain. It’s kind of hard for me to tell where normal thinking ahead ends and worrying starts.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.0, F41.1, F32.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I came in because things have been kind of falling apart since I moved into this busted-up room I’m staying in. Like, it’s not just annoying stuff, it’s gotten to where I’m snapping at people over little things, and I’m not usually like that.\n\nI’ll be up half the night trying to deal with the place — leaks, heat not working right, whatever breaks next — and then I’m dragging myself into work late. I do construction, so being tired and off like that, it matters. I’ve been messing up stuff I normally handle fine, and then I get more pissed at myself, and it just keeps piling up. I guess I finally figured I should talk to somebody before I make things worse.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it sad, exactly. Like, I’m not walking around crying or thinking, “I’m depressed,” you know? It’s more like I’m worn down and irritated a lot, and then I get stuck in my head about the place and how I ended up there.\n\nSome days I do feel kind of low, I guess, especially when I come back and something else is wrong or I realize I’m going to have to deal with the same mess again. But “sad most of the time”… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means like sad all day every day. It’s more like I’m just tired of everything being a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge a lot since you moved into this new room?\n\nPatient: Yeah… on edge is probably a better way to put it than sad. Since I moved into that room, I feel like I’m always kind of braced for the next thing to go wrong. Like I’ll get home from work and instead of relaxing, I’m checking if something’s leaking again, or if the heat’s acting up, or if I’m gonna have to spend half the night trying to patch something just so I can sleep.\n\nAnd then when I don’t sleep right, I go into work already irritated and tired, and I’m late sometimes because I was dealing with the room.
